-> False\nis PyInstaller importable in the venv?    -> No module named 'PyInstaller'\n```\n\nSo the guard reports \"usable\" while the import fails. Both tests then invoke a foreign PyInstaller\nagainst the active environment's code and fail opaquely with a `CalledProcessError` naming\n`release.spec` and a non-zero exit status. Nothing in that message names the real cause.\n\nThe missing behavior: the guard must key on whether `PyInstaller` is importable in the running\ninterpreter, which is what its own skip message already claims to test. This defect bites local developers and any environment that installs only the\ndev lock.\n\n## Scope\n\n- `tests/test_release_spec.py`\n- `tests/integration/test_packaged_executable_assets.py`\n- one new test module holding the regression gate\n\n## Non-Goals\n\n- Adding `pyinstaller` to `requirements-dev.lock` or to the `dev` extra is out of scope. The\ncorrect guard already exists thirty lines away in the same package, at\n`src/counter_risk/compute/limits.py:92`, which raises `ValueError` on non-finite input — and\n`rollups.py` already imports from that module.\n\nDownstream, `src/counter_risk/pipeline/run.py:2101-2107` ranks counterparties with:\n\n```python\nranked = sorted(records, key=lambda record: (-abs(_to_float(record.get(proxy_column))), ...))\n```\n\nNaN compares `False` against every value, so `sorted` cannot order it and the result depends on\ninput arrival order. Executed with three identical counterparty rows in three different orders, one\ncarrying a NaN proxy:\n\n```\norder 1 (NaN first) -> ['ALPHA BANK', 'GAMMA BANK', 'BETA BANK']\norder 2 (NaN last)  -> ['GAMMA BANK', 'BETA BANK', 'ALPHA BANK']\norder 3 (NaN mid)   -> ['BETA BANK', 'ALPHA BANK', 'GAMMA BANK']\ncontrol, same rows with the NaN replaced -> ['GAMMA BANK', 'BETA BANK', 'ALPHA BANK'] both orders\n```\n\nThree different answers to \"which counterparty is our largest exposure\", decided by row order. The\nranking is written to `risk_rankings.csv`, so a wrong and unreproducible number reaches the report.\n\nThe missing behavior is a `math.isfinite` check at the coercion boundary, matching the guard the\npackage already has.\n\nNo existing test can catch it: `tests/compute/test_rollups.py` is 338 lines and contains no `nan`, no `inf`,\nand one negative value. The pipeline also hand-rolls the same coercion inline at\n`src/counter_risk/pipeline/run.py:1731-1790` as `float(x or 0.0)`, where the `or 0.0` fallback is\ninert because NaN is truthy.\n\n## Scope\n\n- `src/counter_risk/compute/rollups.py`\n- `src/counter_risk/pipeline/run.py` — the inline coercion and the ranking sort\n- `tests/compute/test_rollups.py`\n\n## Non-Goals\n\n- Do not change the sign convention or the magnitude handling of any exposure metric. Findings C-2\n  and C-3 are filed separately.\n- Do not substitute `0.0` for a non-finite input anywhere. Coercing a NaN to zero would move the\n  defect from a visibly absurd ranking to a silently wrong one, which is worse.\n- Do not rewire the four unused `rollups` entrypoints in this issue. That is finding C-4 and it\n  changes which code the pipeline executes.\n- Do not add a sort tiebreaker as the fix. The pipeline's `_create_run_directory` implements this contract: `tests/test_pipeline_run_dir.py:165–169` confirms first run lands in `runs/2026-02-13`, second run in `runs/2026-02-13_1`. When `--output-dir` is supplied and the directory is non-empty, the pipeline raises `FileExistsError` (`tests/test_pipeline_run_dir.py:231–240`).\n\n`src/counter_risk/runner_launch.py:115` hard-codes `_000000`: `f\"{resolve_output_root(repo_root, output_root)}\\\\{parsed_date.isoformat()}_000000\"`. `src/counter_risk/gui/runner.py:72` repeats the same literal: `output_root / f\"{month_end.isoformat()}_000000\"`. Both always pass `--output-dir runs/<date>_000000` to the pipeline, bypassing automatic naming entirely. On a second same-month operator run, the pipeline receives a non-empty `_000000` directory and raises `FileExistsError` — the repeat-run protection the pipeline provides can never trigger because the launcher never omits `--output-dir`.\n\nThis is **latent fragility becoming a guaranteed crash** on any same-month re-run: the first run succeeds; the second fails with `FileExistsError` rather than landing in `runs/<date>_1`. Post-run helpers (`Open Output Folder`, `Open Manifest`, `Open Summary`, `Open PPT Folder`) in both VBA and Python also resolve the `_000000` hard-coded path, so after a corrected run they would open a directory that does not exist.\n\n## Scope\n\n- Remove the `_000000` hard-coded suffix from `resolve_output_dir` in `src/counter_risk/runner_launch.py` and `_resolve_output_dir` in `src/counter_risk/gui/runner.py`; either omit `--output-dir` (letting the pipeline's automatic naming run) or implement a shared suffix-safe resolver that mirrors `_create_run_directory` logic.\n- Align `assets/vba/RunnerLaunch.bas` `ResolveOutputDir` with the chosen strategy so VBA and Python agree on what folder name a given operator run produces.\n- Update all post-run open-folder helpers in VBA and Python (`OpenOutputFolder_Click`, `OpenSummary_Click`, `OpenManifest_Click`, `OpenPPTFolder_Click`, and their Python counterparts) so they resolve the actual generated run directory.\n- Update `docs/operator_ux_decision.md`, `docs/gui_runner.md`, and `docs/macro_spec.md` to remove the `_000000` convention and describe the repeat-run-safe naming contract.\n\n## Non-Goals\n\n- Do not redesign the full release packaging or remote trigger flow.\n- Do not change the manifest schema or generated report contents except for the recorded output path if needed.\n- Do not route workflow-template or agent-lane maintenance into this repo.\n- Scaffold-only completion does NOT count: changing the resolver in `runner_launch.py` while leaving `_000000` in `gui/runner.py`, or updating docs without fixing all callers, is a failure. The pipeline's `_create_run_directory` implements this contract: `tests/test_pipeline_run_dir.py:165–169` confirms first run lands in `runs/2026-02-13`, second run in `runs/2026-02-13_1`. When `--output-dir` is supplied and the directory is non-empty, the pipeline raises `FileExistsError` (`tests/test_pipeline_run_dir.py:231–240`).\n\n`src/counter_risk/runner_launch.py:115` hard-codes `_000000`: `f\"{resolve_output_root(repo_root, output_root)}\\\\{parsed_date.isoformat()}_000000\"`. `src/counter_risk/gui/runner.py:72` repeats the same literal: `output_root / f\"{month_end.isoformat()}_000000\"`. Both always pass `--output-dir runs/<date>_000000` to the pipeline, bypassing automatic naming entirely. On a second same-month operator run, the pipeline receives a non-empty `_000000` directory and raises `FileExistsError` — the repeat-run protection the pipeline provides can never trigger because the launcher never omits `--output-dir`.\n\nThis is **latent fragility becoming a guaranteed crash** on any same-month re-run: the first run succeeds; the second fails with `FileExistsError` rather than landing in `runs/<date>_1`. Post-run helpers (`Open Output Folder`, `Open Manifest`, `Open Summary`, `Open PPT Folder`) in both VBA and Python also resolve the `_000000` hard-coded path, so after a corrected run they would open a directory that does not exist.\n\n## Scope\n\n- Remove the `_000000` hard-coded suffix from `resolve_output_dir` in `src/counter_risk/runner_launch.py` and `_resolve_output_dir` in `src/counter_risk/gui/runner.py`; either omit `--output-dir` (letting the pipeline's automatic naming run) or implement a shared suffix-safe resolver that mirrors `_create_run_directory` logic.\n- Align `assets/vba/RunnerLaunch.bas` `ResolveOutputDir` with the chosen strategy so VBA and Python agree on what folder name a given operator run produces.\n- Update all post-run open-folder helpers in VBA and Python (`OpenOutputFolder_Click`, `OpenSummary_Click`, `OpenManifest_Click`, `OpenPPTFolder_Click`, and their Python counterparts) so they resolve the actual generated run directory.\n- Update `docs/operator_ux_decision.md`, `docs/gui_runner.md`, and `docs/macro_spec.md` to remove the `_000000` convention and describe the repeat-run-safe naming contract.\n\n## Non-Goals\n\n- Do not redesign the full release packaging or remote trigger flow.\n- Do not change the manifest schema or generated report contents except for the recorded output path if needed.\n- Do not route workflow-template or agent-lane maintenance into this repo.\n- Scaffold-only completion does NOT count: changing the resolver in `runner_launch.py` while leaving `_000000` in `gui/runner.py`, or updating docs without fixing all callers, is a failure. Therefore the browser demo MUST be one of: (a) **client-side WebAssembly** (stlite/Pyodide/JupyterLite) running entirely in the evaluator's browser with zero data egress; (b) **internal browser-accessible hosting** inside the org perimeter (internal web app / Posit Connect / Streamlit-in-Snowflake / internal Azure app); or (c) an **external demo using only the bundled synthetic fixtures** in `tests/fixtures/`. In every case the LLM chat surface (`src/counter_risk/chat/`) must be DISABLED or gated behind an authorized no-train endpoint — the demo must never call an external LLM with uploaded data.\n\n**Scope** Deliver the lowest-effort viable browser path that meets the constraint, plus a smoke test. Recommended: (1) a `workflow_dispatch` CI job that runs the fixture pipeline and uploads the run folder as a viewable artifact (synthetic-fixture-only, zero real data), AND (2) a thin stlite/Pyodide single-page app committed under `web/` that loads the bundled fixtures and renders the data-quality summary + manifest entirely client-side, with the chat feature compiled out. This follow-up addresses the remaining unmet acceptance criteria.\n\n### Root Cause Analysis\n\nThe keepalive loop logs reveal a cascading failure across multiple systems:\n\n1. **Codex agent checked PR body checkboxes aggressively.** The `.agents/issue-227-ledger.yml` file shows only `task-01` (of 42) was marked `done` in the structured ledger. Yet the PR body went from 42 unchecked → 0 unchecked over 14 iterations. The agent edited the PR body checkboxes directly without updating the ledger, bypassing the structured tracking that was designed to prevent exactly this.\n\n2. **`autoReconcileTasks` amplified the problem.** Each keepalive iteration ran an LLM-based auto-reconciliation step that auto-checked ~1 additional task per run based on loose commit-to-task matching. Over 14 iterations this added up. The reconciler used \"high-confidence\" matching that was insufficiently discriminating — e.g., a commit touching `mapping_diff.py` matched multiple task descriptions simultaneously.\n\n3. **`cascadeParentCheckboxes` may have inflated counts.** The keepalive loop's cascade logic automatically checks all indented child checkboxes when a parent is checked. If the agent or reconciler checked a section-level parent, all sub-tasks under it would cascade to checked.\n\n4. **Codex-as-verifier was self-grading.** Iteration 14 ran a `verify-acceptance` prompt using the same Codex agent that did the implementation work. Despite the prompt explicitly saying \"Do NOT trust checkbox states as evidence of completion,\" the verifier returned `success`. This is a known LLM bias: the agent that produced the work is predisposed to confirm its own output.\n\n5. **The keepalive loop trusted checkbox state as the primary completion signal.** When all 42 boxes were checked AND the verifier returned success AND the CI gate was green, the loop issued `stop (tasks-complete)`. There was no independent mechanical verification of the acceptance criteria.\n\n6. **Scope violation was not mechanically enforced.** The acceptance criterion \"PR diff contains only files matching these patterns\" was a text checkbox, not an automated check. The agent could (and did) check it off despite the diff containing 15+ out-of-scope files.\n\n**Checkbox progression (from keepalive logs):**\n| Time | Unchecked | Checked | Source |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Feb 23, 18:22 | 42 | 0 | Initial |\n| Feb 24, 04:17 | 21 | 21 | Codex (20) + reconciler (1) |\n| Feb 24, 05:29 | 20 | 22 | Codex + reconciler (1) |\n| Feb 24, 07:03 | 12 | 30 | Codex (7) + reconciler (1) |\n| Feb 24, 14:16 | 12 | 30 | No change |\n| Feb 24, 16:06 | 1 | 41 | Codex (10) + reconciler (1) |\n| Feb 24, 16:25 | 0 | 42 | Final reconciler (1) → STOP |\n\n**Progress review at round 5 (07:53) gave alignment score 0.0/10 and recommended STOP** — but the agent continued via `agent:retry` label and eventually checked off all remaining boxes.\n\n## Scope\n\nAddress the concrete gaps identified by both independent verifiers. reconciliation encounters a parsed counterparty whose normalized name is not present in the normalized counterparty mapping, it raises the structured exception type designated for unmapped counterparties (e.g., `UnmappedCounterpartyError`/`ReconciliationException` subtype) and does not raise `ValueError`\n- [ ] The structured unmapped-counterparty exception must expose both `normalized_counterparty` (string) and `raw_counterparty` (string) as direct attributes, and these attributes must return the exact normalized and original counterparty values that triggered the error\n- [ ] In non-strict mode, the same unmapped counterparty input does not raise an exception and the reconciliation result indicates failure/exception presence via the existing non-throwing mechanism (e.g., returned result contains an exception entry) while still using the same structured error type (not `ValueError`)\n\n### Manifest Accounting\n- [ ] `impacted_series` is computed solely from directly missing series and unmapped series; introducing only segment gaps (with no missing/unmapped series) results in `impacted_series == 0`\n- [ ] When there is exactly N directly missing/unmapped series and additionally there are segment gaps present, `impacted_series == N` (segment gaps do not change the count)\n- [ ] Per-sheet `impacted_rows` does not increase due solely to segment gaps when the parent series is not directly missing/unmapped; if only segment gaps exist for otherwise-present/mapped series, per-sheet `impacted_rows == 0`\n- [ ] When a series is directly missing/unmapped, `impacted_rows` for a sheet equals the number of parsed rows that belong to that directly impacted series on that sheet; rows belonging only to other non-impacted series are not counted even if segment-gap logic touches them\n\n### Python Compatibility\n- [ ] Importing `src/**/time_utils.py` does not raise `AttributeError` on Python < 3.11 due to referencing `datetime.UTC` at import time\n- [ ] On Python >= 3.11, `time_utils.UTC is datetime.UTC`; on Python < 3.11, `time_utils.UTC is datetime.timezone.utc` (or documented fallback) and is a tzinfo suitable for aware datetimes\n\n### Pipeline Sheet Keying\n- [ ] `_build_parsed_data_by_sheet` returns parsed-data keys that exactly match the workbook's actual sheet names; it does not introduce a `'Total'` key when corresponds to the parsed output from that sheet (and does not remap other sheets to `'Total'`)\n- [ ] `_build_parsed_data_by_sheet` successfully parses workbooks where different sheets use historical header variants (e.g., synonymous column headers) without falling back to a hard-coded default sheet key; each sheet is parsed using its own header row and returned under its actual name\n\n### `normalize_counterparty` must return identical string output when called multiple times with the same input string within a single test run, verified by calling the function 100 times with the same input and asserting all outputs are equal\n\n## Implementation Notes\n\n### Advisory Items\nThe following items are noted for awareness but are not blocking for this issue:\n\n- **Counterparty normalization mismatch warnings:** Currently emitted whenever normalized parsed counterparties are not found in normalized historical headers. This treats \"unmapped counterparty\" as \"not present in historical headers\" rather than \"not present in the normalized counterparty mapping\" (as the acceptance criteria states). If the mapping and historical headers are not the same concept in this codebase, this could be a conceptual mismatch.\n- **Stack trace preservation:** The `_raise_with_context` helper in `_extract_historical_series_headers_by_sheet` re-raises exceptions with modified messages, which could potentially lose stack trace information in some Python versions.\n- **Performance considerations:** The impacted row/series counting logic in `_count_rows_for_impacted_entities` iterates through all records multiple times, which could have performance implications for large datasets.\n\n## Related\n- Original PR: #191\n- Parent issue: #190","closedAt":"2026-02-24T14:46:39Z","labels":[{"id":"LA_kwDORPCqTs8AAAACX8Y9vQ","name":"agent:codex","description":"Assign to Codex agent","color":"0e8a16"},{"id":"LA_kwDORPCqTs8AAAACX8bCNQ","name":"agents:formatted","description":"Issue has been formatted to AGENT_ISSUE_TEMPLATE","color":"1D76DB"},{"id":"LA_kwDORPCqTs8AAAACX8bCiQ","name":"agents:apply-suggestions","description":"Applies optimization suggestions from agents:optimize","color":"0E8A16"},{"id":"LA_kwDORPCqTs8AAAACX9BWLg","name":"status:in-progress","description":"","color":"ededed"},{"id":"LA_kwDORPCqTs8AAAACX9C7fw","name":"from:codex","description":"PR was created by or for Codex","color":"0e8a16"},{"id":"LA_kwDORPCqTs8AAAACX9weKw","name":"follow-up","description":"","color":"ededed"}],"number":221,"state":"CLOSED","title":"[Follow-up] Modify the strict/fail mode counterparty reconcili (PR #191)","url":"https://github.com/stranske/Counter_Risk/issues/221"},{"body":"## Why\n\nPR #170 addressed issue #39, but verification still found gaps (verdict: **CONCERNS**) around lingering external references in generated PPTX files, brittle chart/object replacement, and error handling that can silently mask PDF/COM failures. This follow-up implements package-level relationship scrubbing, strengthens the static distribution path, improves replacement fallbacks, introduces an explicit `export_pdf` toggle, and adds COM-free automated tests to prevent regressions.\n\n## Source\n- Original PR: #170\n- Parent issue: #39\n\n## Tasks\n- [ ] Implement PPTX relationship scrubber: after generating the distribution PPTX, open it as a zip package and remove/neutralize any external relationships (e.g., external hyperlinks, OLE/external data links) from relevant *.rels parts (presentation.xml.rels and slide*.xml.rels). Re-zip to produce the final distribution PPTX.\n- [ ] Add automated test that builds a distribution PPTX, inspects it as a zip, parses all *.rels files, and asserts there are no Relationship elements with TargetMode=\"External\" (and no known external/OLE relationship types).\n- [ ] Wire the existing `_rebuild_pptx_from_slide_images` into the \"static distribution\" generation path so that, when enabled, the output deck is rebuilt entirely from slide images (one image per slide) with matching slide count.\n- [ ] Refactor chart/object replacement logic to eliminate sole reliance on (slide index, shape name). Add a robust fallback: if per-shape replacement cannot confidently match, default to full-slide image replacement for that slide (or full-deck rebuild when static mode is enabled).\n- [ ] Introduce a dedicated configuration toggle for PDF export (e.g., `export_pdf=true/false`) independent of `distribution_static`, and update the workflow to honor it.\n- [ ] Change PDF export error handling: replace warning-only/suppressed exceptions with explicit error logging and propagate exceptions when `export_pdf=true` (so CI can fail). Add a unit test that simulates an export failure and asserts the error is raised/logged.\n- [ ] Remove `contextlib.suppress` (or equivalent blanket suppression) around COM automation cleanup; replace with targeted exception handling that logs unexpected COM cleanup failures (and optionally re-raises in debug/test mode). Add a unit test that verifies cleanup exceptions are not silently swallowed.\n- [ ] Add PNG integrity validation before inserting slide images into the rebuilt/static PPTX (e.g., verify file exists, non-zero size, and can be opened/verified via Pillow). Add a test that feeds a corrupted/empty PNG and asserts a clear error is raised.\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n- [ ] After generating a distribution PPTX, every relationships part in the final .pptx zip package under `ppt/_rels/*.rels` and `ppt/slides/_rels/slide*.xml.rels` contains zero `<Relationship>` entries with `TargetMode=\"External\"`.\n- [ ] The relationship scrubber removes or neutralizes any external hyperlink relationships such that no `.rels` file contains a `<Relationship>` whose `Type` equals either `http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/officeDocument/2006/relationships/hyperlink` or `http://schemas.microsoft.com/office/2006/relationships/oleObject` while also having `TargetMode=\"External\"`.\n- [ ] An automated test exists that builds a distribution PPTX fixture and fails if any `.rels` file in the output deck contains external relationships (per the rules in the test), without using COM automation.\n- [ ] When `distribution_static` (or equivalent static-mode flag) is enabled, the static distribution generation path calls `_rebuild_pptx_from_slide_images` and produces an output PPTX with the same slide count as the source deck used to generate it.\n- [ ] When static mode is enabled, each slide in the rebuilt PPTX contains exactly one inserted slide image (one per slide) and contains zero chart shapes (`MSO_SHAPE_TYPE.CHART`) in the rebuilt output.\n- [ ] If per-shape replacement cannot confidently match a target (e.g., duplicate/missing shape name for a given slide), the implementation replaces that entire slide with a slide image (or triggers full-deck rebuild when static mode is enabled) rather than leaving the original live object in place.\n- [ ] A new configuration option `export_pdf` (boolean) exists, is parsed from the configured source (e.g., CLI/env/config file), and is used to decide whether PDF export is attempted; PDF export is not implicitly enabled/disabled by `distribution_static`.\n- [ ] When `export_pdf=true` and PDF export fails (exception thrown by the export implementation), the distribution run raises an exception (non-zero exit in CLI usage) and logs an error message that includes the phrase `PDF export failed` and the underlying exception message.\n- [ ] When `export_pdf=false`, PDF export failures (even if the exporter is accidentally invoked) must not be silently swallowed without a log entry: the code must either not call the exporter or, if called, must log a clear warning/error stating export is disabled or skipped.\n- [ ] No blanket exception suppression remains around COM cleanup: `contextlib.suppress(Exception)` (or equivalent that suppresses broad exceptions) is not used in COM cleanup paths; unexpected cleanup exceptions are logged (at error or warning level) with the exception type.\n- [ ] PNG integrity validation is performed before inserting slide images: for each PNG path, the code checks (1) file exists, (2) file size > 0, and (3) Pillow can open and `verify()` the image; any failure raises a specific exception (e.g., `ValueError` or custom) whose message contains the failing file path.\n- [ ] When a corrupted/empty PNG is provided to the static rebuild path, PPTX generation aborts before writing the final distribution PPTX, and the raised error references PNG validation (e.g., contains `invalid PNG` or `PNG validation failed`).\n\n## Implementation Notes\n- Relationship scrubber:\n  - Target package parts: `ppt/_rels/presentation.xml.rels` and all `ppt/slides/_rels/slide*.xml.rels` (and consider scanning all `ppt/**/_rels/*.rels` for safety if feasible).\n  - Use `zipfile` to read/write the PPTX as a package; key(s).\n- [ ] When `parsed_data` is valid, reconciliation proceeds and produces the same outputs as before for a baseline happy-path fixture (no false validation failures).\n- [ ] `time_utils` uses root, regardless of any `config.output_root` value\n- [ ] A single execution does not create multiple sibling run directories (e.g., no `runs/2026-02-13_1/`); all artifacts are contained within `runs/<YYYY-MM-DD>/`\n\n### Manifest Portability and Correctness\n- [ ] `runs/<YYYY-MM-DD>/manifest.json` is valid JSON and parses successfully into a Python object\n- [ ] Every file path recorded in `manifest.json` is relative (does not start with `/` and does not match `^[A-Za-z]:\\\\`)\n- [ ] Every file path recorded in `manifest.json` resolves to an existing file on disk at the time the manifest is written\n- [ ] Manifest path base is consistent across entries (all run-dir-relative or all repo-root-relative; no mixing within one manifest)\n\n### Historical Workbook Safety\n- [ ] Before appending historical data, the update logic deterministically selects the intended worksheet and validates required header columns; on missing columns it raises `ValueError` (or a dedicated exception) whose message includes the sheet name and missing column names\n- [ ] With multiple sheets present, the update logic does not append to an unintended sheet when the intended sheet is present\n- [ ] Given a valid historical workbook fixture with the required sheet and header columns, the update completes without raising and writes an updated workbook into `runs/<YYYY-MM-DD>/`\n\n### PPTX Screenshot Replacement\n- [ ] When screenshot replacement is enabled with valid screenshot inputs, the PPTX written to `runs/<YYYY-MM-DD>/` is not byte-identical to the input PPTX\n- [ ] When screenshot replacement is enabled with N valid screenshot inputs, exactly N corresponding `ppt/media/*` files in the output PPTX differ from the input PPTX when compared as zip archives, and these are the files corresponding to the target shapes identified for replacement\n\n### PPT Status Reporting\n- [ ] If PPT processing cannot run due to missing dependency or unsupported environment, the pipeline records status `skipped` (not `failed`) and completes without raising solely due to PPT processing unavailability\n- [ ] If PPT processing is attempted and an error occurs, the pipeline records status `failed` and either (a) raises an exception that propagates to the caller, or (b) returns a structured result object with a `failed` status field and error details\n- [ ] If PPT status is recorded in the manifest, the manifest includes a machine-readable PPT status field whose value is one of `success`, `skipped`, `failed`\n\n### End-to-End Integration\n- [ ] End-to-end run for `as_of_date=2026-02-13` produces `runs/2026-02-13/manifest.json`, at least one updated historical workbook output, and one PPTX output under `runs/2026-02-13/`\n- [ ] End-to-end: the produced manifest contains only relative paths and all referenced files exist for artifacts created in `runs/<YYYY-MM-DD>/`\n- [ ] End-to-end: with an invalid historical workbook fixture, the pipeline fails before writing an updated workbook\n- [ ] End-to-end: with PPT screenshot replacement enabled and valid screenshot fixtures, the output PPTX under `runs/<YYYY-MM-DD>/` differs from the input PPTX fixture (not a direct copy)\n\n## Notes\n\n**Advisory (non-blocking):** The `_merge_exposure_rows` function in `dropin_templates.py` silently overwrites values on type conversion errors, which could mask data quality issues.\n\n<details>\n<summary>Background (previous attempt context)</summary>\n\n**Failed approach:** Using `config.output_root/<as_of_date>` for output directory  \n**Why it failed:** Violates the hard requirement that outputs must be under `runs/<as_of_date>/` and can misplace artifacts depending on user configuration.  \n**Do instead:** Override/ignore `config.output_root` for this pipeline entrypoint and always resolve run dir to `runs/<as_of_date>/` relative to repo root.\n\n**Failed approach:** Only copying the original PPTX and logging a warning for screenshot replacement  \n**Why it failed:** Does not modify the deck; the produced PPTX does not contain replaced screenshots.  \n**Do instead:** Actually process the PPTX (load, locate targets, replace embedded images) and write the updated deck to the run directory.\n\n**Failed approach:** Generating manifest paths as absolute paths  \n**Why it failed:** Breaks portability across OS/environments and undermines audit/replay.  \n**Do instead:** Record relative paths (with a single consistent base) and ensure each entry points to a real file that exists at write time.\n\n</details>\n\n---\n\n**Source:** Original PR #69, Parent issue without Python installed.  \n  **What to try instead:** Use a bundled executable (built with PyInstaller) and update the runner script to reference that executable bundled with the release.\n\n- **What failed:** Deduping templates based solely on filename order without handling duplicate names between `templates/` and `tests/fixtures/`  \n  **Why it failed:** This can lead to order-dependent behavior and silent selection conflicts if duplicates exist.  \n  **What to try instead:** Implement conflict detection and enforce a deterministic policy (explicit precedence or error listing
